I’ve been waiting months to find out when this was going to print, and it got to the stores before they even told me! I’m SUPER excited for this story, I had a ton of fun writing it. Your kids are guaranteed to love this book to death unless they’re much more mature than I am (which, I’ll admit, is distinctly possible.) Here’s the blurb from the website: Shimi and his friend Baruch are enjoying some snacks when Baruch finds a strange device. The first time Shimi uses it, he’s transported into a strange world of purple, fibrous plants, and ants the size of automobiles! Named the Thing (by Shimi, of course), the boys soon realize their mysterious device has power beyond any technology they’ve ever seen before! But before they can enjoy the Thing for long, cake factory soldiers with strange weapons attack! Someone else is after the Thing, someone with a fondness for puzzle cubes, pliers, and villainous monologues — and there’s nothing he won’t to do get his hands on it!   Will the boys make it home in time for Shimi to finish his long-awaited homework? What is the goal of the elusive Agent McGregor? What is Department “H”? And is there really another spelling for “H”? NEW! From the author of Power Plays, Shinobi, The Time Savers, and other popular titles. Illustrated by Thiago Del Ponte.

[03/01/2023, 8:28:45 AM] Yonah Klein: Correction: in 18:4 the pasuk describes 1000 רכב and 7000 פרשים. I said that רכב meant cavalry and פרשים were chariots. According to the Stone Tanach, I got it backwards. רכב are chariots and פרשים are horse-men. When it says David hamstrung the chariots, it’s referring to the horses that pulled the chariots.

[11/07/2023, 8:56:33 AM] Yonah Klein: Correction: pasuk 7 has the non-Jewish king Adoni-Bezek say that שבעים מלכים בהונות ידיהם ורגליהם מקצצים היו מלקטים תחת שלחני. 
I interpreted this to mean that he had a pile of 70 sets of severed thumbs and toes of conquered kings gathered under his table. 
Levy Yakov kindly shared a translation that makes much more sense: 70 kings who Adoni-Bezek, and whose thumbs and big toes he had ordered cut off, were forced to eat whatever food they could gather from the spilloff from Adoni-Bezek’s table.

[26/02/2024, 9:43:54 AM] Yonah Klein: With 57 pesukim left, we are on the verge of completing sefer Shmuel. If you stopped listening and are looking to get back into it, this is the perfect time to restart. Sefer Melachim picks up at the end of Dovid’s life, and carries us through the rest of the entire first Beis Hamikdosh era and into the golus. It’s a concise history of the whole era and a great way to jump-start your knowledge of the period.

‎[26/09/2024, 12:31:09 PM] Yonah Klein: It's time to announce... wait for it... (drumroll please...)
my NEW BOOK!
Well, okay. I already announced my new book. Like ten months ago. This isn't about that one. This is a different one. A guy can have more than one new book, y'know...
SIGH
Let's try that again.
HEY EVERYONE! It's time to announce... my NEWEST new book!
(Fanfare and wild cheering in the background)
Yesterday, I and my CCO (Chief Cuteness Officer) spotted it for the very first time in the wild - so fresh, they hadn't even gotten it on the shelf yet.
Mayday is a high-octane comic about a 13 year old boy and his family, who struggle to keep safe when strange flashing lights knock their plane out of the sky. They crash land in the middle of nowhere and have to hike to safety - but they're not alone in this faraway corner of nowhere...
Illustrated in brilliant watercolor by the talented Dov-Ber Cohen, this is a fascinating, quick-paced romp through a place so far off the beaten track, even the setting sun had to stop and ask for directions. And it all began so nicely - just a fun plane ride as a bar mitzvah gift. Then came the emergency, the shouting, the fast action - and an unanswered call of MAYDAY!
Mayday is available in the US, England, and Israel, wherever, y'know, "fine Jewish books are sold."

[31/10/2025, 8:59:37 AM] Yonah Klein: At the end of yesterday’s morning, we came to the pasuk ושמתי כסאי בעילם והאבדתי משם מלך ושרים, “I will set My Throne in Eilam, and I will destroy from there king and ministers.” 
We quoted the Gemara in Megillah 10b where it identified the “king” as Vashti, and the “ministers” as Haman and his sons, making this a reference to the Purim story. 
But we asked on this, because Eilam seemed to be a party of Medea, not Babylon. So how can this pasuk apply to the downfall of Vashti (a Babylonian) and Haman of Amalek?

Looking into it a little more, I found that Rashi on the Gemara notes that Shushan was in Eilam, so at least there’s that. But the Maharsha went much further. He says clearly that Eilam is a part of Medea, and this prophecy referred to the downfall of Medea by the hands of the Greeks (particularly Antiochus), years after the Purim story. However, as that story spent include the destruction of a king and ministers, the Gemara is applying this one pasuk to an unrelated miracle that took place in Eilam (i.e. in Shushan) much earlier. 
The fact that the pasuk refers to the Purim story as Hashem putting His Throne in Eilam therefore alludes to the idea that as scary as the Purim story must have been in the moment, the truth is that the Shechina was right there the whole time

This morning’s learning is dedicated lilui nishmas @⁨Levey (Jack) Yakov⁩ Yaakov ben Yaakov, who passed way yesterday and was a dedicated listener to this shiur. As his illness progressed, he told me that he often did not have the strength to learn anything long or involved, and this shiur was a great comfort to him in giving him the opportunity to keep learning. As a result of that conversation, I pushed harder to make sure the recording went out every day I could, and so he has a direct chelek in some of these shiurim being made. HKBH should therefore see his zchus in these shiurim and give him the tremendous reward he is due. 
I also want to apologize for those days I did miss, including yesterday. ‎<This message was edited>

[25/05/2026, 9:02:59 AM] Yonah Klein: For the purpose of today’s recording, I thought it would be a good idea to write down a few value conversions we can refer back to as we go:

*1 eifa* = 3 seah

*1 chomer* (a.k.a. 1 kur) = 10 eifah or 30 seah. A chomer of barley is _worth_ 50 silver shekels. 

*1 letech* = 1/2 chomer = 5 eifa = 15 seah. A letech of barley worth 25 shekels. ‎<This message was edited>

[06/08/2026, 9:50:44 AM] Yonah Klein: Michah 2:8-13
I wonder if perhaps another interpretation of the final pasuk is as follows:
עלה הפורץ לפנינם:
A poretz traditionally refers to someone who doesn’t follow the rules or halachah (i.e. the pasuk ופורץ גדר ישכנו נחש). So by that interpretation, it might mean that prior to the geulah, a brazen sinner will come to Eretz Yisrael. 
פרצו ויעבורו:
And many Jews will follow in his path and sin greatly (פרצו) as they come (ויעבורו) to Eretz Yisrael. But ultimately
ויעבור מלכם לפניהם וה׳ בראשם:
They will do teshuvah, Moshiach will lead a more proper return (ויעבור) to the Holy Land, and the Shechinah will be before us. 

Please note that this is pure speculation, and even if it’s true it wouldn’t be an endorsement of any person or group that willfully sins. But it’s prophecy, and this seems to be a realistic prediction that matches what we’ve seen so far.

The last pasuk mentions the stones in the wall and the splinters of wood crying out that they are stolen. From here, the gemara (Taanis 11a) writes that the very walls of a persons home will testify against their sins on the day of judgment. Something to think about as we approach the Yomim Noraim.

‎[13/10/2026, 10:41:47 AM] Yonah Klein: ‎<attached: 00003647-AUDIO-2026-10-13-10-41-46.opus>
[13/10/2026, 10:44:23 AM] Yonah Klein: Zechariah 5:1-8
The Radak writes that sealing the eifah with the lead weight is an allusion to the idea that the galus that came after the second Mikdash was destroyed would be very long. Worth noting that Zechariah is delivering this prophecy at the start of the second Mikdash era, in a time of hope and optimism, so it’s interesting that Hashem needed us to know from the start that it was only temporary. 
We’ll iy”H come back to this final pasuk tomorrow with a Medrash from Yoma 69b
